Tris‐(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate induces depression‐like behaviors and neurotoxicity by oxidative damage and cell apoptosis in vitro and in vivo.Adult mice daily exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO for 30 days, 2 doses (5 and 50 mg/kg/day, gavage), and studied for activity (OF) and depression (tailed suspension test, forced swim test). Neural mechanism were studied in mice brain and in vitro (Sh‐SY5Y cells) for apoptosis and oxidative stress. Overexpression of proapoptotic proteins and upregulation of oxidative stress markers were observed in hippocampus at the highest dose of 50 mg/kg bw per day. In vitro, TDBP‐TAZTO (0, 12.5, 25 and 50 μM, 48 h of exposure) damaged SH‐SY5Y cells and induced cell apoptosis and oxidative stress in a dose‐dependent manner Ye et al. ( 2015 ). Tris‐(2,3‐Dibromopropyl) Isocyanurate, a New Emerging Pollutant, Impairs Cognition and Provokes Depression‐Like Behaviors in Adult Rats.Adult male rats daily exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO for 6 months, 2 doses (5 and 50 mg/kg per day, gavage). Animals were tested for activity (OF), depression (forced swim test) and spatial learning and memory (Morris water maze). Oxidative stress, apoptosis, synaptic plasticity and inflammation were explored in rat hippocampus. ACTH and corticosterone were measured to assess stress level of the animals. Dose‐dependent increases in hippocampal pro‐apoptosis proteins and pro‐inflammatory cytokine levels and reductions in synaptic plasticity markers (BDNF and synaptophysine) and GSH and SOD antioxidant actors were observed. Histological and immunohistochemical results indicated that TDBP‐TAZTO caused significant increases in the number of apoptotic cells and GFAP‐positive cells, and lead to marked neuronal damage in hippocampal area. These results demonstrated that 6‐month TDBP‐TAZTO exposure impaired hippocampal neurons relatively to the hippocampal‐related behavioural disturbances reported in the same study. Krivoshiev et al. ( 2016 ). Assessing in‐vitro estrogenic effects of currently‐used flame retardants. Toxicology in Vitro 33, 153‐162 TDBP‐TAZTO was investigated for its potential endocrine‐disruptive activity using the MCF‐7 flow cytometric proliferative assay. MCF‐7 human breast adenocarcinoma cells were exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO 100 μM for 24 h and assessed for cytotoxicity and proliferation. The results showed the significant anti‐estrogenic activity of TDBP‐TAZTO when co‐exposed with 17β‐estradiol that remained limited compared to control and to the effects induced by other BFRs tested. Li et al. ( 2016 ). Effects of tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate on steroidogenesis in H295R cells.The effects of TDBP‐TAZTO (0.1‐100 μM) on steroidogenesis and adrenocortical function were studied in vitro using the H295R cell line. The effects of co‐exposure with the cAMP inducer forskolin 10 μM were also studied. Estradiol and testosterone production and steroidogenesis‐related gene expression were measured after 72 h of exposure. The results showed the absence of cytotoxicity and the significant reduction in the hormone production level only at the dose of 100 μM. Adding forskolin with TDBP‐TAZTO strongly increased the production of both hormones compared to control. TDBP‐TAZTO alone did not change the level of expression of all genes studied whereas it blocked forskolin‐induced mRNA expression of specific adrenal steroidogenic enzymes (StAR, 3bHSD2, CYP17, CYP21, CYP19). The present results suggest the ability of TDBP‐TAZTO to be reprotoxic. Cao et al. ( 2018 ). Anti‐estrogenic activity of tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate through disruption of co‐activator recruitment: experimental and computational studies. Archives of Toxicology 92, 1471‐1482The potential anti‐estrogenic activity of TDBP‐TAZTO has been studied using the MCF‐7/BUS cell proliferation assay (E‐Screen bioassay) and the MVLN cells/ERα luciferase reporter gene assay (MVLN assay). TDBP‐TAZTO significantly inhibited 17β‐estradiol‐induced cell proliferation in the E‐Screen assay in a dose range between 10‐9 and 10‐7 M. The luciferase activity induced by 17β‐estradiol measured in the MVLN assay was also significantly inhibited by TDBP‐TAZTO in a dose‐dependent manner. Moreover, neither TDBP‐TAZTO nor 17β‐estradiol affected proliferation of the ERα‐negative breast cancer cell line MDA‐MB‐231. These experimental results confirmed that TDBP‐TAZTO has anti‐estrogenic effects by affecting the ERα‐mediated signalling pathway. Bajard et al. ( 2019 ). Prioritization of hazards of novel flame retardants using the mechanistic toxicology information from ToxCast and Adverse Outcome Pathways.In vivo toxicological data were collected on 62 flame retardants to establish five prioritisation categories based on data availability and toxicological concern. The authors classified TDBP‐TAZTO as having ‘scarce toxicological data indicating toxicological concern’. Bajard et al. ( 2021 ). Endocrine disrupting potential of replacement flame retardants‐Review of current knowledge for nuclear receptors associated with reproductive outcomes.The authors reviewed the in vitro activity of 52 BFRs including TDBP‐TAZTO towards five nuclear receptors (oestrogen, androgen, glucocorticoid, progesterone and Ah receptors) associated with reproductive outcomes, retrieving data from open literature and ToxCast/Tox21 programmes. The authors reported that TDBP‐TAZTO was predicted as having an androgenic receptor antagonist activity from 2 QSAR models. Szychowski et al. ( 2021 ). Tris (2,3‐Dibromopropyl) Isocyanurate (TDBP‐TAZTO or TBC) Shows Different Toxicity Depending on the Degree of Differentiation of the Human Neuroblastoma (SH‐SY5Y) Cell Line.The effects of TDBP‐TAZTO on neuronal differentiation were tested in vitro using neuroblastoma SH‐SY5Y cell line as a model of neuronal differentiation. Cells were differentiated for 7 and 14 days using retinoic acid. TDBP‐TAZTO was applicated at doses ranging 1–100 nM and 1‐100 μM and the expression and/or activity of CYP1A1, CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 enzymes, and AhR and TUBB3 genes were assessed 24 h after the application. Cells were also studied for cytotoxicity (resaruzin reduction and LDH activity assays), apoptosis (caspase‐3 activity) and reactive oxygen production (H2DCFDA fluorimetric assay). The results showed a decrease in the ability of TDBP‐TAZTO to induce EROD activity during the differentiation process, which could be an effect of cell differentiation into neurons. Changes of the EROD activity after stimulation of AhR antagonist (CAY10464) and agonist (βNF) correlated with variations in AhR and CYP1A1 expression during differentiation whereas no mRNA expression of CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 was detected in all types of SH‐SY5Y cells (undifferentiated, differentiated for 7 or 14 days). TUBB3 gene expression highly increased in cells differentiated for 7 and 14 days, confirming the effective differentiation of SH‐SY5Y cells into neurons and the absence of effects of TDBP‐TAZTO on cell differentiation. Han et al. ( 2022 ). In vivo and in vitro study on hepatotoxicity of Tris‐(2, 3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate exposure via mitochondrial and death receptor pathway. TDBP‐TAZTO liver toxicity was assessed in vitro using HepG2 cells (Han et al., 2022). Cells were exposed for 72 h to TDBP‐TAZTO (0, 12.5, 25 or 50 μM). The results showed the ability of this compound to induce apoptosis via mitochondrial pathway and death receptor signalling pathway. Bajard et al. ( 2023 ). Anti‐androgenic activity of novel flame retardants in mixtures: Newly identified contribution from tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate (TDBP‐TAZTO). TDBP‐TAZTO has been tested for its androgenic and anti‐androgenic activities alone or in mixture. The interaction of TDBP‐TAZTO with androgenic receptor (AR) was studied employing an in vitro AR‐dependent luciferase reporter gene assay in MDA‐kb2 cells. TDBP‐TAZTO showed a certain degree of cytotoxicity with an IC50 of 62.9 μM. This compound did not activate the Ah receptor in a significant way at concentrations below the IC50 value whereas it was the only one compound tested in the study that exhibited inhibition of dihydrotestosterone‐induced AR receptor activity with an IC50 of 10.8 μM. In addition, TDBP‐TAZTO contributed to anti‐androgenic activity when combined with six other anti‐androgenic FRs and showed a contribution to the overall mixture effect at a lower concentration than its individual effective dose. The present study provided experimental evidence for the anti‐androgenic properties of TDBP‐TAZTO and its endocrine‐disrupting potency. Szychowski and Skóra ( 2023c ). Involvement of peroxisome proliferator‐activated receptor gamma (PPARa) and autophagic pathways in the mechanism of action of the tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate (TDBP‐TAZTO or TBC) flame retardant in the lung adenocarcinoma (A549) cells in vitro.The present study aimed to assess the involvement of the PPARγ receptor and some autophagic proteins (mTOR and p62) in the toxicity of TDBP‐TAZTO in adenocarcinomic human alveolar basal epithelial cell cultures (A549). Cells were exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO (0.001‐100 μM) and assessed for the impact of this xenobiotic on basic cell metabolism parameters (metabolic activity, ROS production, LDH release and caspase‐3 activity) and on the gene and protein expression of CAT, SOD1, PPARγ, mTOR and p62, which are involved in oxidative stress and autophagy processes. The results showed that TDBP‐TAZTO induced cell damages and apoptosis only at the highest micromolar concentrations (10, 50 and 100 μM) whereas TDBP‐TAZTO showed the ability to trigger oxidative stress and affected the mRNA expression of antioxidant enzymes (SOD1 and CAT) at the lower concentrations (1 and 10 μM) than in the case of apoptosis, suggesting that the apoptosis was ROS independent. The pharmacological modulation of PPARγ receptor agonist indicated that TDBP‐TAZTO acts in the A549 cell line probably through activation of the mTOR‐PPARγ pathway and could interfere with the p62 autophagy pathway. Szychowski and Skóra ( 2023b ). Disruption of neurosteroid synthesis and release by tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl)isocyanurate in primary mouse cortical astrocytes in vitro.The study aimed to determine the impact of TDBP‐TAZTO on the neurosteroid (progesterone, testosterone and estradiol) production and secretion as well as the mRNA expression of key enzymes involved in its production in a model of primary mouse astrocyte cell culture. Effects of TDBP‐TAZTO (1 and 10 μM) on hormone production, and gene expression of various enzymes involved in the production and metabolism of such hormones (Actb, Gadph, p53, Mki67, Cyp19a1, Cyp17a1 and Hsd17b4) were measured after 48 h of exposure. Results showed a significant dose‐dependent decrease of the expression of the proliferative marker Ki67. In the same time; TDBP‐TAZTO increased progesterone production with a significant decrease in testosterone production at both concentrations, which is accompanied by a decrease in Cyp17a1 mRNA expression, that is, the main enzyme metabolising progesterone to testosterone. Moreover, TDBP‐TZATO increased progesterone secretion in the culture medium at both concentrations. TDBP‐TAZTO also induced an increase in the production of estradiol at the concentration of 1 μM correlated with the higher level of expression of Cyp19a1 mRNA, the enzyme metabolising testosterone to estradiol. The present in vitro study suggests that TDBP‐TAZTO have endocrine‐disrupting properties with potential consequences for brain development and functioning. Szychowski and Skóra ( 2023a ). Tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl)Isocyanurate has an Effect on Inflammation Markers in Mouse Primary Astrocytes In vitro.The impact of TDBP‐TAZTO on neuroinflammation and apoptosis was tested in a model of primary mouse astrocyte cell culture. Dose‐related effects of TDBP‐TAZTO (1–100 nM and 1–100 μM) on caspase‐1 and ‐3 activities, DNA cell staining, oxidative stress and inflammation‐related proteins were measured after 6, 24 or 48 h of exposure. TDBP‐TAZTO increased caspase‐1 and caspase‐3 activities at the two highest concentrations of 50 and 100 μM. TDBP‐TAZTO also increased the level of such inflammation markers as Cat, IL‐1β and IL‐1βR1 proteins and decreased the level of the proliferation marker (Ki67). No changes in astrocyte morphology and formation of apoptotic bodies were observed. The results suggest the lack of toxicity of TDBP‐TATZO on astrocytes in vitro at the low levels of concentration used. Tabecka‐Lonczynska, Kaczka, and Kaleniuk (2023). Involvement of estrogen receptor alpha (ERÎ±) and impairment of steroidogenesis after exposure to tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate (TBC) in mouse spermatogenic (GC‐1 spg) cells in vitro.The effects of TDBP‐TAZTO 10 μM (48 h of exposure) on the expression of nuclear receptors involved in the formation of steroid hormones, proteins and enzymes responsible for steroidogenesis and the levels of steroid hormones (estradiol, progesterone and testosterone) were tested in the GC‐1 spg cell line as a mouse model of spermatogenic cells in vitro. Effects of co‐exposure to TDBP‐TAZTO with BHPI 10 μM (Erα antagonist) were also studied. The results indicated the ability of TDBP‐TAZTO to increase the Erα mRNA and protein expression whereas Pparγ, AhR and IGF‐1R gene expression were reduced compared to control cells. In addition, a decrease in the levels of most of the analysed proteins and enzymes involved in steroid conversion was observed. A significant decrease was observed in the production and secretion of progesterone to the culture medium compared to the control. Slight non‐significant decreases in estradiol and testosterone were also observed. Co‐exposure with BHPI modulated the effects of TDBP‐TAZTO alone, suggesting the involvement of Era in the mechanism of TDBP‐TAZTO toxicity. These results demonstrated that TDBP‐TAZTO affects the process of steroidogenesis by reducing the expression of proteins and enzymes of major contribution to the synthesis of gonadal hormones, suggesting the endocrine disrupting potency of this compound. Tabecka‐Lonczynska, Skóra, et al. (2023). Reprotoxic Effect of Tris(2,3‐Dibromopropyl) Isocyanurate (TBC) on Spermatogenic Cells In Vitro. Molecules 28, xxx.The effects of TDBP‐TAZTO on metabolic activity (resazurin reduction assay), apoptosis (caspase‐3 activity and protein expression) and signalling key genes (Ki67, p53, Pparγ, Ahr, Esr1 mRNA expression) were evaluated in mouse spermatogonic GC‐1 spg cells. Cells were exposed to increasing concentrations of TDBP‐TAZTO (1, 10, 50, 100 nM and 1, 10, 50, 100 μM) for 48 h. Effects of co‐exposure to TDBP‐TAZTO 10 μM with BHPI 10 μM (Erα antagonist), estradiol 100 nM or letrozole 1 μM (aromatase inhibitor) were also tested. TDBP‐TAZTO (10, 50 or 100 μM) induced significant decreases in resaruzin reduction, and concomitant increases in caspase‐3 activity and expression at levels of 10 and 100 μM. Significant increases in Ki67, p53, Pparγ and Esr1 mRNA expression were observed with TDBP‐TAZTO 10 μM whereas the Ahr expression was decreased compared to controls. An increase in Pparγ mRNA levels and a decrease in Ahr and Esr gene expression were observed in cells co‐exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO and estradiol. These results suggest the significant impact of TDBP‐TAZTO on the dysregulation of the steroid‐related signalling pathway in male reproductive cell line, suggesting the toxicity of this compound on male fertility. Yang, Li, et al. (2023). Effects of fibrous microplastics on the accumulation of tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate and behavior of zebrafish via water‐ and foodborne exposure routes.The present study investigated the uptake and depuration and the behavioural toxicity of TDBP‐TAZTO (5 μg/L) in zebrafish. The effects of co‐exposure with fibrous microplastics were also investigated. Adult female wild‐type zebrafish were exposed to TDBP‐TAZTO (5 μg/L added in artificial freshwater) for 7 days. TDBP‐TAZTO depuration was evaluated after 4 additional days in clean non‐contaminated freshwater. Foodborne exposure to TDBP‐TAZTO was performed through the feeding of zebrafish with clean or contaminated daphnids (10 per fish and day). The accumulation of TDBP‐TAZTO in fish muscle via waterborne exposure increased over time to reach a maximum and comparable concentration at 3 and 7 days of exposure. TDBP‐TAZTO content in muscle was significantly reduced by 60% after the period of depuration. The TDBP‐TAZTO accumulation in zebrafish muscle after 7 days of foodborne exposure was quite low, two magnitude orders lower than waterborne exposure. TDBP‐TAZTO did induce significant behavioural changes related to swimming trajectories, distance and cumulative duration of manic, active and static states of zebrafish whatever the way of exposure (waterborne and foodborne exposure). Tabecka‐Lonczynska et al. ( 2024 ). Unraveling the anti‐androgenic mechanism of tris(2,3‐dibromopropyl) isocyanurate (TBC) via the non‐classical testosterone pathway and steroidogenesis: Potential human reproductive health implications.The effects of TDBP‐TAZTO 10 μM alone or combined with testosterone 10 μM or flutamide 1 μM (anti‐androgen compound) were evaluated in mouse‐derived spermatogonic GC‐1 spg cells after 48 h of exposure. Cells were assessed for their metabolic activity (resaruzin reduction and LDH activity assays), apoptosis (caspase‐3 activity) and a large set of protein expression, involved for a part in non‐classical testosterone pathway and steroidogenesis. TDBP‐TAZTO induced a reduction in metabolic activity and an increase in LDH release, indicating cell damages, while caspase‐3 activity was slightly reduced. TDBP‐TAZTO exposure led to a reduction in progesterone synthesis alone or combined with testosterone or flutamide whereas estradiol synthesis was decreased with TDBP‐TAZTO alone only. Testosterone production remained unaffected in all conditions of exposure. TDBP‐TAZTO exposure downregulated SRC‐EGFR‐AKT‐p33MAPK protein production affecting non‐classical androgen signalling. In addition, TDBP‐TAZTO reduced StaR and HSD3b1 levels suggesting impaired steroidogenesis. TDBP‐TAZTO was able to initiate autophagy through the significant increase in the Beclin1 production level. These results indicate the potential of TDBP‐TAZTO to disrupt steroid hormone synthesis and androgen signalling pathways, to modulate autophagy processes and to affect cell proliferation and survival. Taken together, these results confirm the potential reproductive toxicity of TDBP‐TAZTO.

Combined Transcriptomic and Proteomic Approach to Identify Toxicity Pathways in Early Life Stages of Japanese Medaka (Oryzias latipes) Exposed to 1,2,5,6‐Tetrabromocyclooctane (TBCO).Japanese medaka (Oryzias latipes) embryos were exposed to 10, 100 or 1000 μg/L TBCO from 2 h post‐fertilisation until 1 day post‐hatch. TBCO accumulated 0.43‐1.3 × 104‐fold in embryos with accumulation rates of 1.7–1.8 per day. Exposure to 100 and 1000 μg/L significantly impaired hatching time and success (p < 0.001). Transcriptomic and proteomic analyses of embryos exposed to 100 μg/L revealed enrichment in pathways related to embryo development, hatching, visual perception and cardiac function. Targeted bioassays confirmed these predictions: visual performance was significantly reduced at 10 μg/L (p = 0.013) and 100 μg/L (p = 0.028), while heart rates decreased significantly at 100 μg/L (p = 0.014) and 1000 μg/L (p < 0.001). Cytotoxicity of hexabromocyclododecane, 1,2‐dibromo‐4‐(1,2‐dibromoethyl) cyclohexane and 1,2,5,6‐tetrabromocyclooctane in human SH‐SY5Y neuroblastoma cells.Cytotoxicity of TBCO was studied in human SH‐SY5Y neuroblastoma cells. The brominated flame retardant, TBCO, impairs oocyte maturation in zebrafish (Danio rerio).Sexually mature female zebrafish were given a diet of 35.3 or 628.8 μg TBCO/g food for 14 days after which, stage IV oocytes were isolated to assess maturation in response to 17α,20β‐dihydroxy‐4‐pregnen‐3‐one, aka maturation inducing hormone (MIH). Both doses reduced oocyte maturation. Transcriptomics implicated changes in expression of insulin‐like growth factor signalling and expression of microRNAs regulating this pathway in the reduction of oocyte maturation. Devoy, Raza, Kleiner, et al. (2023). The brominated flame retardant, 1,2,5,6‐tetrabromocyclooctane (TBCO), causes multigenerational effects on reproductive capacity of Japanese medaka (Oryzias latipes). TBCO at low (40.6 μg/g) and high (1034.4 μg/g) doses reduced F1 fecundity by 33.9% and 33.3%, respectively. High‐dose effects persisted in F2 (29.8% reduction), but F3 recovered completely. TBCO caused multigenerational, not transgenerational, reproductive effects. Abundances of mRNA for cholesterol side chain cleavage enzyme (cyp11a), aromatase (cyp19a) and luteinizing hormone receptor (lhr) were differentially expressed in gonads from F1 females. Devoy, Raza, Jones, et al. (2023). Both doses impaired 17α,20β‐dihydroxy‐4‐pregnen‐3‐one, aka maturation inducing hormone (MIH) stimulated oocyte maturation (23.4% and 20.0% reduction). Vitellogenin transcript abundance decreased dose‐dependently, while E2 concentrations remained unchanged. Raza et al. ( 2023 ). Impairment of oocyte maturation as a mechanism of decreased fecundity in Japanese medaka (Oryzias latipes) exposed to the brominated flame retardant, 1,2,5,6‐tetrabromocyclooctane (TBCO).Stage IX oocytes exposed in vitro to 20 and 200 μg/L TBCO showed significantly reduced MIH‐stimulated maturation, while 0 and 2 μg/L had no effect. For in vivo assessment, sexually mature fish were fed control, 100 or 1000 μg/g TBCO‐spiked food for 21 days. Both TBCO concentrations significantly decreased fecundity and oocyte maturation. Plasma 17β‐estradiol (E2) levels and hepatic vitellogenin (vtgI and vtgII) transcript abundances showed no significant differences between treatments.

Protocol for the human risk assessments related to the presence of brominated flame retardants (BFRs) in food

The Annex is provided as a separate pdf file containing the risk assessment protocol applied by the CONTAM Panel to update the previous risk assessments of brominated flame retardants (BFRs) in food.

Computational analysis for the assessment of emerging and novel BFRs

The Annex is provided as a separate pdf file and xls file containing the details and outcome of the similarity matrix, profilers, information sources, QSAR predictions and read across exercise. The files are available on EFSA's Knowledge Junction Community on Zenodo at: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21026968.

Occurrence data on emerging and novel BFRs in food submitted to EFSA, dietary surveys per country and age group, and detailed results of the human chronic dietary exposure assessment

The Annex is provided as a separate excel file and contains the summary statistics of the occurrence data on emerging and novel BFRs in food submitted to EFSA, and data about the dietary surveys per country and age group available in the EFSA Comprehensive Database used in the dietary exposure assessment, as well as the detailed results of the human chronic dietary exposure assessment to emerging and novel BFRs. The file is available on EFSA's Knowledge Junction Community on Zenodo at: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21027469.

Benchmark dose (BMD) modelling

The Annex is provided as a separate pdf file containing the results of the benchmark dose (BMD) analysis.

Raw occurrence data on emerging and novel bfrs in food

The raw occurrence data on the emerging and novel BFRs in food extracted from EFSA Data Warehouse by 8 September 2025 and sampled between years 2010 and 2025 are available on EFSA's Knowledge Junction Community on Zenodo at: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21027554.

Outcome of the targeted consultation on the draft scientific Opinion on the update of the risk assessment on emerging and novel BFRs in food

The Annex is provided as a separate pdf file containing the comments received during the targeted consultation and the replies by the CONTAM Panel, and this is available under the Supporting Information section on the online version of the Scientific output.
